2. RECOGNITION 
C. TSD Mission Moment – Martell Elementary Students 
Students and staff at Martell Elementary are being honored tonight for 
their successful implementation and integration of the Leader in Me 
Program and their development of a mission statement for the school. The 
Leader in Me is based on the “7 Habits of Highly Effective People,” by 
Stephen Covey. Students are taught leadership principles and strategies 
based on the 7 Habits. During the summer of 201 4, teachers conducted a 
book study on this program to determine its viability for our community. 
Team members spent countless hours writing multiple grants securing 
over $50,000 to implement Leader in Me. Over five days of training has 
taken place during t he summer and weekends to prepare teachers, 
support staff, parents, and PTO board members for the implementation of 
this initiative. 
Research indicates the Leader in Me program has positive effects in 
numerous areas including a student ’s self-confidence, responsibility, 
leadership, problem solving, and communication as well as cultivating a 
positive culture. At Martell, the entire community has embraced this 
initiative and results are already being seen. The Troy Board of Education 
is excited about this opportunity to develop and sharpen students’ abilities 
and tonight we are pleased to recognize the following people , after which 
Mr. Lesser will tell us a little more about the work that has taken place:   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Advisors and students will come up as their 
names are called to receive their certificates and then will give a short 
presentation on Leader in Me at Martell. 
ADVISORS: 
Chuck Lesser, Sue Delbeke and Stacey Schoenherr 
STUDENTS: 
(Linda has names)

2.       RECOGNITION 
D. Principal Appreciation Month 
Throughout the State of Michigan, February has traditionally been celebrated as 
Principal Appreciation Month, in recognition of the outstanding work of our 
building-level educational leaders. In Troy, we are fortunate to have an 
exemplary group of principals dedicated to the success of every student in the 
District. This evening, we will pass a resolution to commend our elementary and 
secondary principals for their ex ceptional commitment to excellence in the Troy 
School District.  
WHEREAS, energetic and inspiring school leadership is essential if teachers, 
schools and districts are to ensure that all Troy students are college  and career 
ready; and, 
WHEREAS, K-12 school principals play a vital role in the success of students 
and act as the liaison between the school and the community it serves, ensuring 
that parents and the community are aware of student and school achievements; 
and, 
WHEREAS, Troy School District pr incipals are dedicated,  quality educational 
professionals committed to providing sound leadership and guidance to their 
staff and students; and,  
WHEREAS, during this month, we join with educators, parents and students 
throughout Michigan to raise awaren ess of the importance of educational 
leadership, and to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy for their 
exemplary service; 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED , that the Troy Board of Education 
officially recognizes February as Principal Appreciation Month in Troy and thanks 
our dedicated building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.    
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution. 
  Moved by:     
  Supported by:

2.       RECOGNITION 
E.  Spirit of Diversity Awards  
The Board of Education is proud to recognize eight individuals for our 
Spirit of Diversity Awards.  These awards recognize those who help foster 
a climate of inclusion and diversity that creates an optimal learning 
environment for the entire school community.  
This is the third year for the Spirit of Diversity Awards, which were 
designed by the Troy Div ersity & Inclusion  Council as a way to honor 
those who go above and beyond to ensure that every student is valued 
and diversity is celebrated within the Troy School District. When your 
name is read, please come up to receive your certificate and nomination  
packet.  
Recommended Action:  Kerry Birmingham will read names of the 
winners and the Board will present certificates. 
Nancy Lining —who is being recognized tonight for he r work to create 
and grow a climate of inclusion at Larson Middle School. She serves as the 
chair of the Larson Strong initiative, a character  education program that 
gives students positive messages and life lessons, such as standing up for 
one another and showing resilience. Nancy also led a group of 14 Larson 
students through  the Religious Diversity J ourneys Program, which is 
designed to promote greater understanding, awareness and knowledge of 
the many religions in the metro -Detroit area and prepare students to live 
in an increasingly diverse society.  
Dawn Hayes—who is bei ng honored tonight for her work in fostering a 
climate of inclusion, acceptance and value for all students at Athen s High 
School. She ensures that her room is a place where all students feel 
comfortable and often stop by throughout the school day. Dawn als o 
volunteers her time with the school’s Ethnic Fair, participates as an 
educator with the Troy Parent Advisory Committee’s roundtable 
discussions and facilitates an opportunity for Athens students to Shop with 
a Hero during the holidays. This year, she pla ns to launch a program to 
collect gently used clothing in her classroom so that economically 
10
disadvantaged students can choose what they need in a safe and 
nurturing environment. 
Jen Hilzinger —who is being recognized tonight for her work in three 
different TSD schools: Schroeder, Boulan Park Middle School and Troy 
High School. Jen is a parent volunteer who has served as co -coordinator 
of the Diversity Council, but also lends her time and talents to many 
individual diversity efforts at our schools. She coordinates the Schroeder 
World Tour, which celebrates the cultures and ethnicities of our students 
and staff. At Boulan Park, Jen created and headed the Parent Network, a 
targeted volunteer effort to reach out to ESL parents and get them 
“plugged in” to their  child’s school life. At Troy High, she has been a 
leading voice on many fronts, a strong supporter of the Social Justice 
Project and the coordinator of Fred Korematsu Day, which h onors the 
efforts to fight against Japanese internment during World War II. 
Kerry Brennan—who is being honored tonight for her work in teaching 
compassion and value for all to her students at  Wattles Elementary. Kerry 
uses the book Wonder, which chronicles the life of a boy with facial 
deformities, to teach her fifth grade students the importance of looking at 
what’s inside a person as the true measure of their character. When the 
class had finished the book, Kerry invited a young student with similar 
facial issues to speak with the class. This visit —along with the thoughtfu l 
discussions that took place in the class during this time —created a climate 
of inclusion and caring. Kerry was nominated by a parent who said she 
saw her own son grow a heightened awareness and compassion through 
this process. 
Lori Medina-Yashinsky—who is being recognized tonight for her work 
in organizing and leading a group called the Cultural Connection at 
Leonard Elementary School . Under her direction, this group has set up 
activities that celebrate the rich ethnic diversity of the school. As a paren t 
volunteer, Lori has also organized a cultural family picnic at Boulan Park 
and this year is spearheading a two day event at Leonard where students 
will be able to wear customary clothing from their ethnic background and 
teach others about the history and customs that country represents.   
Lisa Carruthers—who is being honored tonight for her creation of the 
peer-to-peer program at Leonard Elementary called “Leopard Links.” This 
program has created an environment of inclusion not just for students 
with Auti sm, which was the original intention, but for ALL students at 
11
Leonard. Lisa attended training and dedicated many hours of her own 
time designing and implementing this program, which has been a huge 
success. All students have learned tolerance and empathy a nd it can be 
seen in many ways every day at Leonard. Lisa  was nominated by the 
parent of a student with ASD who says that she—and this program—made 
all the difference for her son in school.  
Stephanie Sacco —who is being recognized tonight for her work to 
promote diversity and inclusion at three schools: Leonard Elementary, 
Smith Middle School and Troy High School . As the cheer coach at Smith 
Middle School, Stephanie continually teaches empathy, compassion and 
inclusion of all students. She has organized fr ee tumbling classes for 
students who could not afford lessons, mentored students who needed a 
place to belong and created a cohesive team and family atmosphere with 
33 girls from different races, ethnicities and economic backgrounds. 
Through her example, t he young athletes are learning values that go far 
beyond cheer. Stephanie is also a frequent volunteer in classrooms at 
Leonard and lends her time to the Troy High Boosters club so that all 
students have the opportunity to participate in activities. 
Nadeen Brown—who is being honored tonight for her work at Leonard 
Elementary School, but also for her tireless efforts on behalf of diversity 
throughout the Troy School District. Nadeen works with the staff at 
Leonard to help them learn to interact with new and  existing families on a 
cultural level. She also presents at new teacher orientation each year, 
guiding our newest teachers on the importance of understanding our 
students’ cultural backgrounds. One of Nadeen’s greatest contributions to 
the district is her  leadership, development and management of the Troy 
African American Parent Network, which offers free Saturday tutoring to 
students and many opportunities for families to connect. Nadeen was 
nominated by her fellow teachers at Leonard, who say she has a h uge 
impact on students, staff and our community. 
On behalf of the Board of Education, we thank all of these wonderful 
Diversity Champions for their wonderful work in our TSD community.

 7. A.   03.03.15 
7. CURRICULUM 
A. CONSIDERATION:   Schools Closed to Open Enrollment for  
       2015/2016 
Administration has reviewed the enrollment for elementary, middle, and high 
school attendance areas.  Due to the elementary buildings ’ total student 
enrollment and physical space constraints, we recommend that Bemis and 
Schroeder Elementary Schools be closed to open enrollment.  Our goal for 
Athens and Troy High  has been to maximize each building’s  enrollment, 
which has resulted in  both schools being closed to open enrol lment for the 
past several years. Based on current enrollment projections f or the 
2015/2016 school year , it is recommended that Athens and Troy High  be 
closed to open enrollment with the exception of not more than 25  incoming 
ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High School attendance area, but 
wish to attend Athens High School .  Applications for the 25 spots at Athens 
High School will be accepted from March 4 - March 31, 2015. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
Be it therefore resolved that the Troy Board of Education adopts a resolution to 
close Bemis Elementary and Schroeder Elementary  School as well as  Athens High and 
Troy High School to open enrollment for the 201 5/2016 school year, with the exception 
of not more than 25 incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High School 
attendance area , but wish to attend Athens High School.  Students with siblings 
attending Athens High School and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be allowed to 
open enroll to Athens for the 2015/2016 school year.  
Yes: __________ 
No: __________

 7. B.   03.03.15 
7. CURRICULUM 
B.  REPORT:  Oakland County Special Education Programming 
Changes in special education programming at the county level include 
demographic changes, state performance plan targets, instructional 
changes in general education, and changes in a chievement expectations 
and accountability.  These changes are the result of the Oakland County 
Center Program Redesign.   The following report  will provide a  brief 
overview of the factors that have affected o ur current services and 
supports for students with disabilities in Troy.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report is presented by Mrs. 
Shawna Veit, Supervisor of Special Education. 
85

Troy School District students with complex needs here, at home, in the Troy

LRE:  More Time in General Education for 
More Students with IEPs?
Placement decisions are made for each child by his or her IEPT, however, a number of factors 
are ‘encouraging’ an increase in GE for core instruction, including…
• Changes in instruction in General Education:
• Increase in direct strategy instruction in GE for  both core and non-
core subjects
• Increase in/emphasis on differentiation and explicit, scaffolded 
instruction for those who need it in some core areas
• Workshop model for literacy instruction at the elementary level 
includes guided reading groups at each child's ‘instructional level’ 
• Changes in specials scheduling at the elementary level has 
107
contributed to an increase in schedule consistency for core subjects 22
LRE:  More Time in General Education for 
More Students with IEPs?
Placement decisions are made for each child by his or her IEPT, however, a number of factors 
are ‘encouraging’ an increase in GE for core instruction, including…
• Changes in instruction provided in Special Education
• 99% of students with disabilities are provided some ‘on-grade-level’ math instruction at 
the elementary level (location varies depending on student need)
• Emphasis on including special education teachers in general education professional 
learning opportunities
• Shifting from an ‘alternate program’ approach to a ‘targeted intervention’ approach for 
both literacy and math at the elementary level
• Addition of math support courses at the middle school level
• Restructuring of RR math courses at the high school level to facilitate success in Algebra I 
(and beyond) or  success in math related courses aligned with the student’s EDP

113
• Local revenue not significantly impacted in this amendment.
• State revenue increased 7.6% as a result of using actual figures from the State Aid Status Report 
into amendment figures.  The following were contributing factors :
1. 22a Prop A Obligation:  $2,491,157
$50 increase in per pupil foundation allowance
2. 22b Discretionary Payment: $631,434
3. 147C MPSERS UAAL Rate Stabilization Categorical (4.56% to 7.63%):  $3,306,660
4. 22f Best Practice Incentive Categorical ($52 to $50): ($27,907) 
• Federal Grants amended to reflect actual award amounts and PY revenues received
• Incoming Transfers & Other Transactions increased to account for closing of the Durant Fund -         
$5,619,125
• Out of District Tuition decreased to account for amounts expected to be received within 60 days 
of year-end: ($394,235)
• Special Ed Public Act 18 revenue decreased to reflect actual amount per statement: ($192,962)

• Reconciled Salary Accounts to reflect current staff salaries.

A RFP was created to obtain competitive bids for future Mechanical Equipment Needs 
as part of the Troy School Distric t Bond Program.  The scope of work for this RFP is 
to provi de pricing for Chiller & Unit Ventilator Equipment so that equipment 
selections/design can be completed and equipment costs are established for budget 
purposes.  Bid Category 236426 is specifically for Air Cooled Rotary Screw Water 
Chillers.  This equipment selection and associated pricing will be used for the MEP 
work at Troy High, Troy Athens, Larson Middle and Boulan Park Middle. 
The RFP resulted in 4  bids for Bid Category 236426 , Daikin/McQuay was the low 
qualified bidder  and at this time I am requesti ng approval for purchase of Chiller 
Equipment per the attached bid .  The total project cost  requested for award is 
$420,800.00 (breakdown per site is below) .  ThermalNetics is the distributor for 
Daikin/McQuay and the purchase order will be issued to them directly . The funding 
for this purchase will be from the 2014 Capitol Projects Fund. 
Athens High School - $210,400.00 
Boulan Park MS - $105,200.00 
Larson MS - $105,200.00 
If you have any questions, please let me know.

 B.  FIRST READING:   Revisions to Board Policy B-0102 Board  
   Members’ Term of Office, B-1202 Place of 
   Meeting and B-1204 Quorum  
 The Board has established a process to review current Board policies 
and recommend updates, if necessary, during monthly Board workshops. 
Members of the Board of Education have reviewed and proposed 
changes to update the following policies:   
   B-0102   Board Members’ Term of Office 
   B-1202   Place of Meeting 
   B-1204   Quorum  
 The first reading of these policies is presented at this evening’s meeting.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Board members will read changes to Board policies  
B-0102, B-1202, and B-1204 in a round-robin fashion.  
144
   SCHOOL DISTRICT ELECTIONS     B‐0100 
B‐0102  BOARD MEMBERS’ TERM OF OFFICE 
The school electors shall elect members of the Board of Education 
for a term of six (6) years, commencing with the general school 
election in November of 2009 2016 and on the November general 
election date in the odd even years thereafter. 
Adopted:  January 15, 2008 
Revised: 
145
   MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1202  PLACE OF MEETING 
Meetings of the Board are held at the District Administrative 
Centers, unless otherwise announced and posted unless 
otherwise announced at least twenty‐four hours in advance and 
notice posted in accordance with laws governing the District. 
Adopted:  April 8, 1975 
Revised:  March 15, 1977 
  January 16, 1979 
  April 15, 2003 
146
   MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1204  QUORUM 
Four  members  of  the  Board  constitute  a  quorum  for  the 
transaction of business.  If a member of the Board is not physically 
present at a duly held Board m eeting where a quorum is already 
present, the Board member who is  not physically present at the 
meeting may participate via remo te participation by means of 
telephone, teleconference, video co nference and/or other form of 
electronic means available to the Bo ard and that member as long as 
any attendee of the meeting can hear  and interact with Board member 
not in physical attendance. 
Absent Board member must have permission of presiding chair to 
participate remotely but may not vote. 
All meetings of the Board must comp ly with the Open Meetings Act, 
MCL 15.261 et seq, as that Act may be  amended from time to time.  
In the event of a conflict between this Policy and the Act, the Act 
shall control. 
Adopted:  January 16, 1979 
Revised:  July 10, 2012 
147
 10. C.       03.03.15 
10. OTHER 
 C.  FIRST READING:   Revisions to Board Policy B-1211 Public  
   Participation in Board of Education Meetings 
 Members of the Board of Education have reviewed and proposed changes  
to update Board Policy B -1211 Public Participation in Board of Education 
Meetings.   
 The first reading of this policy is presented at this evening’s meeting.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  One Board member will read changes to Board policy 
B-1211 Public Participation in Board of Education Meetings.  
148
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1211  PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
The  Board  values  public  participation  and  recognizes  the  importance 
of  public  input  on  issues  affecting  the  Board  and  the  District.   This 
Policy  has  been  adopted  to  balance  the  importance  of  public 
participation  with  other  important  objectives,  including:  insuring  all 
community  members  a  fair  and  reasonable  opportunity  to  express 
their  views  to  the  Board;  completing  Board  business  effectively, 
efficiently and in  an  orderly manner; respecting other important  time 
commitments of Board members, District staff and audience members; 
and,  respecting  the  rights  of  Board  members,  District  staff,  students, 
and audience members. 
Public  participation  will  be  limited  to  60  minutes  per  Board  meeting.  
Generally,  Public  participation  at  the  beginning  of  a  meeting  will  be 
limited to agenda items that require action by the Board of Education 
at  the  current  meeting.   These  agenda  items  are  posted  as 
‘Consideration.’   All  other  agenda  items  and  any  non‐agenda  items 
will  be  addressed  during  public  participation  at  the  end  of  the 
meeting.there  will  be  30  minutes  of  public  participation  at  the 
beginning  of  the  meeting,  limited  to  agenda  items.   There  will  be  an 
additional 30 minutes of public participation at the end of the meeting, 
limited to non‐agenda items. 
Although the public participation portion of the meeting is limited, an 
exception may be made, if necessary, so that no one’s right to address 
the Board will be denied. 
Audience  members  who  wish  to  address  the  Board  must  complete  a 
Speaker Request Form.  Forms will be available 30 minutes prior to the 
start  of  the  meeting  and  must  be  completed  and  submitted  to  the 
Secretary  of  the  Board  of  Education  before  the  start  of  the  meeting.  
The  Board  President  or  presiding  officer  will  recognize  audience 
members for public participation subject to the following rules: 
 Each  person  wishing  to  speak  must  identify  self  by  name  and 
address. 
 Speakers will be taken in the order forms are collected. 
 Recognized speakers may be permitted to speak for up to three (3) 
minutes.  However, the President of the Board may reduce the time 
149
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1211  PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
limit  for  each  speaker  in  order  to  accommodate  all  of  the 
individuals who wish to address the Board of Education. 
 Recognized  speakers  may  not  transfer  or  delegate  their  time  to 
others. 
 Delegations  who  wish  to  express  their  views  are  encouraged  to 
  select one speaker to present the delegation’s views to the   Board. 
 Each  member  of  a  delegation  wishing  to  speak  must  submit  a 
  separate Speaker Request form. 
 Persons who have completed Speaker Request Forms but have not 
had  an  opportunity  to  address  the  Board  or  to  complete  their 
presentations may be given the option of submitting their views in 
writing to the Secretary of the Board of Education, whose name and 
e‐mail  address  are  printed  on  each  Speaker  Request  Form.   The 
Secretary of the Board will forward all such written presentation to 
all Board members. 
 Speakers  shall  direct  their  comments  to  the  Board  and  not  to 
District staff or other audience members. 
 Speakers  should  not  expect  Board  members  to  answer  questions 
during  public  participation.   Questions  will  be  referred  to  the 
Superintendent  for  review  and  study  and,  if  appropriate,  future 
Board action. 
 Speakers  are  expected  to  express  themselves  responsibly  and 
courteously  and  with  due  respect  for  the  fact  their  views  and 
opinions  may  not  be  shared  by  all  present.   The  following  are 
examples of irresponsible or discourteous expression:  
 Denigrating  Board  members,  District  staff,  students,  or  other 
audience members. 
 Speculating  on  the  motives  of  Board  members,  District  staff,  
students, or other audience members. 
 Complaints  about  District  staff  or  students  that  have  not  been 
brought to the District’s attention through appropriate channels. 
150
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B‐1200 
B‐1211  PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
 Speakers  may  not  express  complaints  against  Board  members  or 
District staff unrelated to their offices or jobs. 
 Speakers  may  not  express  complaints  against  Board  members  or 
District  staff  that  are  related  to  their  offices  or  job  unless  the 
affected  person  or  persons  have  been  notified  and  permitted  a 
reasonable opportunity to choose whether to hold a hearing on the 
complaint in open or closed session. 
 Audience members are expected to conduct themselves responsibly 
and  courteously  and  with  due  respect  for  the  fact  their  views  and 
opinions  may  not  be  shared  by  all  present.   Thus,  raucous  or 
disrespectful  expressions  of  agreement  or  disagreement  by 
audience members are not appropriate. 
 The  Board’s  President  or  presiding  officer  is  charged  with  the 
responsibility  for  insuring  that  public  participation  assists  the 
Board in discharging its responsibilities and is conducted according 
to  this  Policy.   Thus,  the  Board  President  or  presiding  officer  is 
authorized  to  enforce  this  Policy  by,  among  other  things: 
interrupting  presentations  to  remind  speakers  and  audience 
members of this Policy; dismissing previously recognized speakers 
who  violate  this  Policy;  requesting  speakers  or  audience  members 
to  leave  the  meeting  if  they  violate  the  Policy  and,  if  necessary, 
requesting  the  assistance  of  law  enforcement  officers  to  assist  in 
removing  speakers  or  audience  members  who  refuse  to  leave;  or, 
adjourning the meeting. 
The  Board  may  amend  or  waive  this  Policy,  or  specific  provisions 
thereof,  when,  in  the  Board’s  judgment,  amendment  or  waiver  is 
appropriate. 
Revised:  April 1988
